Q1. A packing job has 24 boxes. The team packs 3 boxes daily at first for 5 days, and then 9 boxes daily. How many days are needed in all?
A. 6 days
B. 10 days
C. 9 days
D. 4 4/5 days
Answer: 6 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_multi_phase_join_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
\sum r_it_i=W
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=3\times 5
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=5+\frac{9}{9}=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 6 days

---

Q2. 20 people have food for 45 days. After 5 days, there are 25 people. For how many additional days will the food be sufficient?
A. 29 days
B. 27 days
C. 40 days
D. 32 days
Answer: 32 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_food_remaining_stock
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{900-20\times 5}{25}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=32
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 32 days

---

Q3. 24 people have food for 30 days. If the number of people becomes 25, for how many days will the food last?
A. 26 4/5 days
B. 28 4/5 days
C. 34 4/5 days
D. 36 days
Answer: 28 4/5 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
24\times 30=25\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=28.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{24\times 30}{25}
\]
Answer: 28 4/5 days

---

Q4. A finishes a job in 20 days and B in 12 days. What is the ratio of work done by A and B in the same time?
A. 32:12
B. 12:32
C. 20:12
D. 3:5
Answer: 3:5
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_work_done_ratio_from_times
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
W_A:W_B=\frac{1}{20}:\frac{1}{12}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
W_A:W_B=12:20
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
W_A:W_B=3:5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=3:5
\]
Answer: 3:5

---

Q5. One pipe fills a tank in 12 hours, while another empties it in 36 hours. In how many hours will the tank be completely filled with both open?
A. 16 hours
B. 27 hours
C. 18 hours
D. 14 hours
Answer: 18 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_basic_fill_empty
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_f-r_e}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
r=1/12-1/36
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 18 hours

---

Q6. A, B and C work on successive days in that order. They pack 8, 5 and 3 boxes respectively on their turns. If 120 boxes are to be packed, in how many days will the job be completed?
A. 22 days
B. 27 days
C. 26 2/5 days
D. 23 days
Answer: 22 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_alternating_group_cycle
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=8+5+3
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=22
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 22 days

---

Q7. Workers build 15 metres of boundary each day, while 3 metres are spoiled daily. In how many days will 150 metres be completed?
A. 10 days
B. 16 1/2 days
C. 12 1/2 days
D. 10 1/2 days
Answer: 12 1/2 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_negative_positive_mixed_work
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=\frac{150}{15-3}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=12.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 12 1/2 days

---

Q9. A can finish \(\frac{1}{3}\) of a work in 8 days. In how many days can A finish all of it?
A. 2 2/3 days
B. 29 days
C. 24 days
D. 11 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_work_fraction_days
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T=d\times \frac{q}{p}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=8\times \frac{3}{1}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=8\div \frac{1}{3}=24
\]
Answer: 24 days

Q10. The efficiencies of A and B are in the ratio 4:7, and the efficiencies of B and C are in the ratio 7:8. What is the ratio A:B:C?
A. 4:7:8:3
B. 4:7:8
C. 8:7:4
D. 4:7:8:2
Answer: 4:7:8
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_efficiency_chain
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
B_c
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
LCM(7,7)=7
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
A:B:C=4:7:8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A:B:C=4:7:8
\]
Answer: 4:7:8

---

Q11. A and B finish a work together in 12 days, and their efficiency ratio is 3:4. How many days will A alone need?
A. 19 days
B. 16 days
C. 28 days
D. 9 days
Answer: 28 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_efficiency_ratio_scaling
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
W=(a+b)T
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
W=(3+4)\times 12
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_A=\frac{84}{3}=28
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(3+4)12}{3}=28
\]
Answer: 28 days

---

Q12. One man packs 4 boxes in a day and one woman packs 3 boxes in a day. If 6 men and 8 women work together, in how many days will they pack 240 boxes?
A. 6 days
B. 5 days
C. 1 day
D. 7 1/2 days
Answer: 5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_group_conversion_and_or
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T=\frac{W}{\sum ne}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=\frac{240}{6\times 4+8\times 3}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=W/R
\]
Answer: 5 days

Q13. Machines A, B and C operate for 6 hours and make 12, 15 and 18 parts per hour. How many parts are produced?
A. 265 items
B. 269 items
C. 337.5 items
D. 270 items
Answer: 270 items
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_machine_breakdown_midway
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
O=\sum r_it_i
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
O=12\times 6+15\times 6+18\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
O=270
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O=(r_1+r_2+r_3)t
\]
Answer: 270 items

---

Q14. Pipe A can fill a tank in 12 hours and pipe B can fill it in 18 hours. Both pipes are used, and Pipe A is closed after 2 hours. In how many hours will the tank be completely filled?
A. 22 1/2 hours
B. 18 hours
C. 11 hours
D. 15 hours
Answer: 15 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_pipe_closed_before_completion
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=(r_A+r_B)t_1,\quad T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(3+2)\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{26}{2}=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_B}
\]
Answer: 15 hours

---

Q15. A, B and C can finish a job together in 8 days. A and B together need 20 days. How many days will C alone take?
A. 16 days
B. 13 1/3 days
C. 10 1/3 days
D. 9 1/3 days
Answer: 13 1/3 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_pairwise_total_abc
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_X=r_{team}-\sum r_i
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
r_X=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_X=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 days

Q16. A machine makes 15 parts per day initially for 4 days, then 12 parts per day. How many days are required for 180 parts?
A. 14 days
B. 9 days
C. 17 1/2 days
D. 13 days
Answer: 14 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_productivity_decay
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-r_1t_1}{r_2}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=4+\frac{180-15\times 4}{12}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=14
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+t_2
\]
Answer: 14 days

Q17. Normally a pipe fills a tank in 12 hours, but with leakage it fills in 17 hours. What is the emptying time of the leak?
A. 46 4/5 hours
B. 41 4/5 hours
C. 51 hours
D. 40 4/5 hours
Answer: 40 4/5 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{17}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=40.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 40 4/5 hours

---

Q18. 18 workers finish a job in 8 days, working 5 hours daily. How many workers should work 6 hours daily to finish it in 12 days?
A. 13 workers
B. 10 workers
C. 27 workers
D. 18 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_painting_walls_area_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
18\times 8\times 5=M_2\times 12\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{18\times 8\times 5}{12\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

---

Q19. A job needs 30 days to finish. After 6 days of work, what part of the job is still left?
A. 4/5
B. 4/1
C. 1/5
D. 5/4
Answer: 4/5
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_remaining_work_fraction
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
R=1-\frac{t}{T}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
R=1-\frac{6}{30}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
R=\frac{24}{30}=4/5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
R=\frac{30-6}{30}=4/5
\]
Answer: 4/5

---

Q20. 80 of 300 forms are complete. 10 workers are available and 8 days remain. How many additional workers are required?
A. 18 workers
B. 15 workers
C. 27 workers
D. 22 workers
Answer: 18 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{220}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-10
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 18 workers

---

\]

Q25. One-fourth of a tank is filled. The pipe can fill a full tank in 12 hours. In how many hours will the remaining part be filled?
A. 7 hours
B. 6 3/4 hours
C. 4 hours
D. 9 hours
Answer: 9 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_partial_tank_initially_filled
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{1-f}{r_f}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
T=\frac{1-1/4}{1/12}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=(1-f)F
\]
Answer: 9 hours

Q27. A can do a work in 12 days and B in 18 days. After A works for 4 days alone, both work together. In how many days is the work completed?
A. 11 days
B. 10 4/5 days
C. 3 4/5 days
D. 8 4/5 days
Answer: 8 4/5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=15\times 4,\quad R=180-60
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{120}{15+10}=8.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{180-15\times 4}{15+10}
\]
Answer: 8 4/5 days

\]

\[

\]

\[
T=16\frac{1}{3}

Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=C_f+t_f
\]
Answer: 16 1/3 hours

Q37. 18 workers working 5 hours per day finish the job in 8 days. If the daily working time is 6 hours, how many workers are needed to finish it in 12 days?
A. 10 workers
B. 27 workers
C. 13 workers
D. 18 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_man_days_hours_basic
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
18\times 8\times 5=M_2\times 12\times 6
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{18\times 8\times 5}{12\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q38. Food is sufficient for 30 people for 30 days. After 6 days, the number of people becomes 40. For how many more days will the food last?

\]

Q40. For the same work, AB finishes in 10 days, BC in 12 days and CA in 15 days. In how many days can A alone finish it?

C. 24 days
D. 21 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_pairwise_ab_bc_ac
Explanation:

\[
2(A+B+C)=(A+B)+(B+C)+(C+A)
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
AB=10,\ BC=12,\ CA=15
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_A=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A=\frac{AB+AC-BC}{2}
\]
Answer: 24 days

Q43. A work requires 15 workers for 16 days with 6 working hours per day. How many workers will be needed for 12 days with 8 hours per day?
A. 20 workers

C. 15 workers
D. 18 workers

\[

\]

\[
15\times 16\times 6=M_2\times 12\times 8
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{15\times 16\times 6}{12\times 8}=15
\]
Answer: 15 workers

Q46. A and B can separately complete a work in 8 and 6 days. A is absent for the last 2 days and B is absent for the last 3 days. In how many days is the work completed?
A. 10 days
B. 7 days
C. 6 days
D. 4 4/5 days
Answer: 6 days
Difficulty: Hard

\[

\]

T=6
\]

\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 6 days

Q47. A and B together complete a work in 6 days. Their efficiencies are in the ratio 2:3. In how many days can A alone complete it?

Explanation:

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Q48. If A can complete a work in 6 days and B can complete it in 12 days, how many days are needed when both work at the same time?
A. 18 days
B. 9 days
C. 6 days
D. 4 days
Answer: 4 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(6,12)=12
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=12/6=2,\quad B=12/12=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{12}{2+1}=4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{6\times 12}{6+12}=4
\]
Answer: 4 days

Q49. A and B work together at first. A's time is 8 days and B's time is 12 days. If A leaves after 4 days, when will the job be finished in all?

\]
Answer: 6 days

Q51. A job earns ₹12000 for A and B. If their work shares are in the ratio 8:12, what is A's wage?

\]

Q55. A filling pipe fills a tank in 12 hours and a leak empties it in 36 hours. In how many hours will the tank be completely filled when both work together?
A. 16 2/3 hours
B. 13 1/3 hours
C. 8 1/3 hours
D. 12 1/3 hours
Answer: 13 1/3 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 hours

Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 9 days

Q75. A work requires 18 workers for 8 days with 5 working hours per day. How many workers will be needed for 12 days with 6 hours per day?
A. 10 workers
B. 18 workers
C. 27 workers
D. 13 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_farm_harvest_workers
Explanation:

\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2

\[
18\times 8\times 5=M_2\times 12\times 6
\]

\[
M_2=10
\]

\[
M_2=\frac{18\times 8\times 5}{12\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q77. In a joint job, A alone needs 18 days and B alone needs 6 days. A is not present in the last 2 days and B in the last 3 days. How many days does the job take?
A. 7 1/4 days
B. 5 4/5 days
C. 5 1/4 days
D. 11 1/4 days
Answer: 7 1/4 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:

\[

\]

\[

\]

T=7.25
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 7 1/4 days

Q78. The efficiencies of A and B are in the ratio 4:5. Together they complete a work in 10 days. In how many days can A alone complete the work?

D. 12 1/2 days
Answer: 22 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_relative_efficiency_percent
Explanation:

\[
W=(a+b)T
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
W=(4+5)\times 10
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_A=\frac{90}{4}=22.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(4+5)10}{4}=22.5
\]
Answer: 22 1/2 days

Q79. A can complete a piece of work in 12 days and B can complete the same work in 24 days. In how many days will they complete it working together?
A. 8 days
B. 12 days
C. 36 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 8 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(12,24)=24
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=24/12=2,\quad B=24/24=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{2+1}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{12\times 24}{12+24}=8
\]
Answer: 8 days

Q80. A and B work together at first. A's time is 24 days and B's time is 6 days. If A leaves after 4 days, when will the job be finished in all?
A. 1/2 days
B. 5 days
C. 2 days
D. 6 1/4 days
Answer: 5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(5+20)\times 4
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{20}{20}=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 5 days

Q82. A and B divide ₹7200 according to the work-share ratio 8:12. What will A receive?

C. ₹2880

Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_wage_partial_time

\]

Q86. A tank is filled by a pipe taking 12 hours and emptied by another pipe taking 36 hours. In how many hours will the tank be filled if both are opened?

D. 12 hours
Answer: 12 hours

\]

\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 12 hours

Q111. A job needs 24 days to finish. After 10 days of work, what part of the job is still left?

\]

Q116. A takes 24 days to finish a work and B takes 6 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?
A. 30 days
B. 6 days
C. 15 days
D. 4 4/5 days
Answer: 4 4/5 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(24,6)=24
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=24/24=1,\quad B=24/6=4
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{1+4}=4.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{24\times 6}{24+6}=4.8
\]
Answer: 4 4/5 days

Q117. A, B and C complete a job and receive ₹9000. Their work shares are in the ratio 30:30:12. What is C's share?

B. ₹1500
C. ₹1503
D. ₹1200
Answer: ₹1500
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_wage_with_helper
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
Share=\frac{c_i}{\sum c}\times W
\]
Use the work-share ratio for the wage.
\[
Share=\frac{12}{72}\times 9000
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
Share=1500
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
Share=contribution\ ratio\times wage
\]
Answer: ₹1500

Q118. Pipe A fills a tank in 12 hours. A drain pipe can empty the tank in 36 hours. If both are open, in how many hours will the tank be completely filled?

B. 30 hours
C. 26 hours
D. 45 hours
Answer: 30 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:

\[

\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{15}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=30
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 30 hours

W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=18\times 2,\quad R=180-36
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{144}{18+6}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2+\frac{180-18\times 2}{18+6}
\]
Answer: 8 days

\[

\]

Q161. A alone takes 10 days and B alone takes 15 days. During a joint job, A does not work in the last 2 days and B does not work in the last 3 days. What is the total time?

Q162. Two workers A and B have efficiencies 3:4. If both together finish the job in 12 days, in how many days can A alone finish it?

\[

\]

\]

\[

\]
Answer: 28 days

Q163. A alone finishes a job in 10 days, while B alone finishes it in 30 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?

C. 20 days
D. 40 days
Answer: 7 1/2 days

Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:

\[
W=LCM(10,30)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/10=3,\quad B=30/30=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{3+1}=7.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{10\times 30}{10+30}=7.5
\]
Answer: 7 1/2 days

\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]
Answer: 30 hours

Q185. 12 workers complete the same job in 10 days at 8 hours a day. How many workers are required to complete the same job in 16 days at 6 hours a day?
A. 10 workers
B. 13 workers
C. 19 workers
D. 12 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_farm_harvest_workers
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
12\times 10\times 8=M_2\times 16\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{12\times 10\times 8}{16\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q186. A and B can separately complete a work in 15 and 30 days. A is absent for the last 2 days and B is absent for the last 3 days. In how many days is the work completed?
A. 9 1/4 days
B. 7 1/3 days
C. 12 1/3 days
D. 10 1/3 days
Answer: 12 1/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
8(T-2)+4(T-3)=120
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=12\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 12 1/3 days

Q187. A and B can separately complete a job in 20 days and 30 days. What is the time taken when they work together?
A. 12 days
B. 50 days
C. 25 days
D. 20 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(20,30)=60
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=60/20=3,\quad B=60/30=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{60}{3+2}=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{20\times 30}{20+30}=12
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q188. A and B start the same job. A alone takes 20 days and B alone takes 18 days. After 5 days only B continues. How many days are needed in all?

\[

\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.

T=5+\frac{170}{20}=13.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=5+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 13 1/2 days

Q192. Pipe A can fill a tank in 12 hours and pipe B can empty the same tank in 36 hours. If both pipes are opened together, in how many hours will the tank be filled?
A. 20 hours
B. 16 hours
C. 30 hours
D. 23 hours
Answer: 20 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:

\[

\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{20}
\]

\[
B=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 20 hours

Q227. An inlet pipe fills a tank in 15 hours. With a leak, the same tank fills in 21 hours. In how many hours can the leak empty a full tank?
A. 48 1/2 hours
B. 51 1/2 hours
C. 63 hours
D. 52 1/2 hours
Answer: 52 1/2 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{15}-\frac{1}{21}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=52.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 52 1/2 hours

Q228. 10 workers complete the same job in 15 days at 8 hours a day. How many workers are required to complete the same job in 20 days at 6 hours a day?
A. 15 workers
B. 13 workers
C. 14 workers
D. 10 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_painting_walls_area_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
10\times 15\times 8=M_2\times 20\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{10\times 15\times 8}{20\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q229. A group is expected to complete a work in 15 days. It has already worked for 5 days. What fraction remains?

Q230. 8 workers have completed 48 of 240 forms. Only 12 days are left. How many extra workers are needed to finish on time?
A. 4 workers
B. 8 workers
C. 11 workers
D. 12 workers
Answer: 8 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{192}{1\times 12}\right\rceil-8
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 8 workers

\[

\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[

\]

\[
T=2+\frac{120-12\times 2}{12+4}
\]
Answer: 8 days

\[

\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=T_{active}+2=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_a+t_i
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q262. A and B work on a job but leave before it is completed. A leaves 2 days early and B leaves 3 days early. A alone takes 12 days and B alone takes 10 days. How long does the job take?
A. 6 days
B. 6 2/5 days
C. 12 days
D. 8 days
Answer: 8 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
20(T-2)+24(T-3)=240
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 8 days

Q263. A and B can separately complete a job in 12 days and 6 days. What is the time taken when they work together?
A. 4 days
B. 9 days
C. 18 days
D. 6 days
Answer: 4 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:

\[
W=LCM(12,6)=12
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=12/12=1,\quad B=12/6=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{12}{1+2}=4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{12\times 6}{12+6}=4
\]
Answer: 4 days

Q295. Normally a pipe fills a tank in 10 hours, but with leakage it fills in 20 hours. What is the emptying time of the leak?
A. 21 hours
B. 25 hours
C. 26 hours
D. 20 hours
Answer: 20 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[

Q327. A and B can separately complete a job in 30 days and 18 days. What is the time taken when they work together?

\]

\[

\]
Answer: 11 1/4 days

Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=24/24=1,\quad B=24/6=4
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{1+4}=4.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{24\times 6}{24+6}=4.8
\]
Answer: 4 4/5 days

Q333. A can finish a work in 30 days and B in 24 days. They work together for 3 days, then B alone finishes it. How many days are taken altogether?

D. 16 1/5 days
Answer: 21 3/5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:

\[

\]

\[
W_1=(4+5)\times 3
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.

\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 21 3/5 days

Q336. A alone finishes a job in 24 days, while A and B together finish it in 6 days. How many days will B alone take?

Q345. A tank is filled by a pipe in 12 hours. When a leak is present, it takes 22 hours. How many hours will the leak alone take to empty the tank?

B. 21 2/5 hours
C. 25 2/5 hours
D. 33 hours
Answer: 26 2/5 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:

\[

\]

\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{12}-\frac{1}{22}
\]

\[
L=26.4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]
Answer: 26 2/5 hours

D=\frac{S-consumed}{people}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{600-30\times 8}{40}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 9 days

Q370. Two workers have efficiencies 8:24. What is the ratio of days required by them separately?
A. 3:1
B. 24:32
C. 32:24
D. 8:24
Answer: 3:1
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_time_ratio_from_efficiency
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T_A:T_B=E_B:E_A
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T_A:T_B=24:8
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_A:T_B=3:1
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3:1
\]
Answer: 3:1

Q371. A and B can separately complete a job in 8 days and 12 days. What is the time taken when they work together?
A. 8 days
B. 4 4/5 days
C. 10 days
D. 20 days
Answer: 4 4/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(8,12)=24
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=24/8=3,\quad B=24/12=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{3+2}=4.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{8\times 12}{8+12}=4.8
\]
Answer: 4 4/5 days

Q372. A and B start a work together. A can complete it in 30 days and B in 24 days. After 5 days, A leaves. In how many days will the work be completed in all?
A. 26 days
B. 25 days
C. 23 days
D. 20 days
Answer: 20 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(4+5)\times 5
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=5+\frac{75}{5}=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=5+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 20 days

Q374. A repair team completes 18 metres per day and daily damage removes 3 metres. In how many days will 150 metres be completed?

B. 12 1/2 days
C. 12 days
D. 10 days
Answer: 10 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_digging_filling_negative_work

\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
T=\frac{150}{18-3}
\]

\[
T=10
\]

\[

\]

Q386. A, B and C work on successive days in that order. They pack 10, 5 and 3 boxes respectively on their turns. If 72 boxes are to be packed, in how many days will the job be completed?
A. 18 days
B. 15 days
C. 12 days
D. 13 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_conditional_activation
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=10+5+3
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 days

Q388. A group is expected to complete a work in 15 days. It has already worked for 6 days. What fraction remains?

Q389. 12 workers have packed 120 boxes out of 360. To finish the rest in 12 days, how many additional workers are needed?
A. 4 workers
B. 6 workers
C. 8 workers
D. 12 workers
Answer: 8 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{240}{1\times 12}\right\rceil-12
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 8 workers

\]

\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{900-30\times 6}{36}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S_l}{P_2}
\]
Answer: 20 days

Q407. The efficiencies of A and B are in the ratio 30:12. What is the ratio of the times taken by A and B for the same work?

Q408. A can complete a piece of work in 15 days and B can complete the same work in 12 days. In how many days will they complete it working together?
A. 13 1/2 days
B. 6 2/3 days
C. 27 days
D. 12 days
Answer: 6 2/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(15,12)=60
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=60/15=4,\quad B=60/12=5
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{60}{4+5}=6\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{15\times 12}{15+12}=6\frac{2}{3}
\]
Answer: 6 2/3 days

Q409. A and B work together at first. A's time is 20 days and B's time is 30 days. If A leaves after 4 days, when will the job be finished in all?
A. 21 days
B. 24 days
C. 29 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_forward_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W=(r_A+r_B)t_1+r_Bt_2
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(9+6)\times 4
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{120}{6}=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{W-W_1}{B}
\]
Answer: 24 days

Q411. A team adds 15 metres to a project each day, but 3 metres are lost each day. What is the time for 120 metres?
A. 10 days
B. 8 days
C. 15 days
D. 12 days
Answer: 10 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_digging_filling_negative_work
Explanation:

\[
T=\frac{W}{r_+-r_-}
\]

\[
T=\frac{120}{15-3}
\]

\[
T=10

\]

\]

Q418. 12 workers have completed 96 of 240 forms. Only 10 days are left. How many extra workers are needed to finish on time?
A. 3 workers
B. 1 worker
C. 9 workers
D. 3.75 workers
Answer: 3 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_added_to_meet_deadline
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{144}{1\times 10}\right\rceil-12
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=3
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 3 workers

Simplify the relation to get the answer.

Q452. A and B can separately complete a job in 8 days and 24 days. What is the time taken when they work together?
A. 16 days
B. 6 days
C. 8 days
D. 32 days
Answer: 6 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(8,24)=24
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=24/8=3,\quad B=24/24=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{3+1}=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{8\times 24}{8+24}=6
\]
Answer: 6 days

Q453. A food stock lasts 30 days for 20 people. How many days will it last for 25 people?
A. 19 days
B. 24 days
C. 26 days
D. 30 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
20\times 30=25\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{20\times 30}{25}
\]
Answer: 24 days

R=\frac{20-2}{20}=9/10
\]
Answer: 9/10

Q484. A team requires 15 working days for a job. If work is interrupted for 3 days, what is the total duration?

B. 18 days

Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_interrupted_work
Explanation:

\[

\]

\[
T_{active}=\frac{540}{30+6}

Q486. If A can complete a work in 18 days and B can complete it in 30 days, how many days are needed when both work at the same time?
A. 11 1/4 days
B. 24 days
C. 48 days
D. 18 days
Answer: 11 1/4 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(18,30)=90
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=90/18=5,\quad B=90/30=3
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{90}{5+3}=11.25
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{18\times 30}{18+30}=11.25
\]
Answer: 11 1/4 days

\]
